## thematic

- `9` **DRAM Supply to Remain Tight in 2027, Prompting NVIDIA to Lower HBM Configurations for Rubin Ultra, Says TrendForce - TrendForce**
  TrendForce says DRAM supply stays tight through 2027 and, as a consequence, NVIDIA is lowering HBM configurations on Rubin Ultra — i.e., the memory shortage is now binding enough to change accelerator specs, not just prices. That is the inflection framing: for 000660 (and Samsung/Micron on samsung-hbm/hbm4) it signals sustained HBM pricing power and sold-out capacity into 2027, but it caps HBM content per GPU, so HBM revenue growth leans more on price/mix than on stacks-per-package. For rubin-ramp it implies Rubin Ultra bandwidth/capacity per package comes in below prior roadmap expectations, which suggests NVIDIA has to lean harder on NVLink-scale-up and system-level bandwidth to compensate — indirectly a positive for scale-up fabric content (mrvl-fabric, UALink/NVLink Fusion narratives) and for custom XPUs that can trade HBM for cheaper memory tiers. Confirming evidence: a formal Rubin Ultra spec sheet showing fewer HBM4E stacks or lower per-stack capacity, and 2026–27 HBM contract prices settling up; refuting evidence would be NVIDIA reaffirming original HBM capacity, or SK hynix/Micron guiding to volume-led rather than price-led HBM growth. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMicEFVX3lxTFBpMEw0SW9oV0d6aWVLVHBSOG5GTG9tUktYQmpyTDNjVnN6QkhWdjFQeGMxT0xHQmJGOGpza0liR1pETkJUNUIyTDNYUHhUTG05NEthRnlZblF1alcxbmttc2xaaVdLTlV3YlJJTkFPVGg?oc=5)
- `8` **SK Hynix, $Micron Technology (MU.US)$ , and Samsung have sold out of 2027 capacity for DRAM/HBM. $SanDisk (SNDK.US)$, Samsung, Micron current annual NAND capacity has been sold out, with Kioxia and SK Hynix expected to finalize allocations by - Moomoo**
  Per an aggregated Moomoo note, SK hynix, Micron and Samsung have sold out 2027 DRAM/HBM capacity, and SanDisk, Samsung and Micron have sold out current annual NAND capacity, with Kioxia and SK hynix expected to finalize allocations shortly. If accurate, this is the clearest pricing-power datapoint available: sold-out 2027 DRAM means the cycle is contracted two years forward, removing the usual spot-price mean-reversion risk and letting suppliers price 2026–27 LTAs at their discretion — directly bullish 000660, 285A and SNDK on ASP and mix, and inflationary for every buyer of memory (NVDA/AMD/AVGO/MRVL accelerator BOMs, hyperscaler capex per rack). Watch the disclosed 2026 capex range and any stated TPU deployment volume as the confirming datapoint. *(headline only)*

- `8` **Aehr Test Systems Receives Follow-On FOX-XP Order | AEHR Stock News**
  Aehr received a follow-on production order from its lead silicon photonics WLBI customer for a fully automated FOX-XP with nine WaferPak blades at up to 3,500W each, plus Auto Aligner and robotic wafer handling — but shipment is guided to 1H calendar 2027, so this is backlog, not near-term revenue, and no dollar value was disclosed. The stated driver is capacity expansion for next-gen silicon photonic ICs across transceivers, optical switching, co-packaged optics, LPO and chip-to-chip optical I/O, with Erickson emphasizing that integrating/co-packaging laser sources makes pre-package latent-defect screening and laser stabilization a 'foundational' step — that mechanism is what turns photonics volume into a per-wafer tool attach for Aehr. This is the closest thing to an inflection datapoint in today's set for wlbi-test and cpo: a repeat production (not qualification) order plus management's claim of rising global engagement 'spanning engineering qualification systems, production capacity planning, and next-generation device roadmaps' suggests CPO/optical-I/O volume manufacturing is being planned for 2027, which is corroborative for LITE, AAOI, IQE, AVGO/MRVL optical roadmaps. Confirm with a second named WLBI customer or a disclosed order value/backlog step-up at the next print; refute if orders stay concentrated in one customer and the 2027 ship date slips, which would push Aehr's photonics revenue inflection out another year.

  Reports that China has begun mass production of domestic DUV lithography machines triggered a 2,030-point drop in the Taiwan index. If substantiated, this is the wfe-china inflection the coverage watches: domestic DUV would blunt the marginal effectiveness of export controls, let SMIC/CXMT/YMTC expand mature and DRAM/NAND capacity without ASML supply, and structurally cap the China revenue line at ASMI, AMAT, LRCX, KLA and TER over time while adding supply to the nand-cycle. Caution on sourcing: this is a single low-reliability outlet reporting 'mass production' without tool specs, overlay/throughput data, or customer acceptance — Chinese DUV claims have repeatedly conflated prototype shipment with production-worthy tools of record. - `8` **Global Market Watch: Advanced Packaging Takes the Stage**
  Two packaging inflection datapoints: TSMC and Intel are both pushing CoPoS glass-substrate packaging with commercial validation targeted in 2026 and mass production by 2028, claiming material utilization rising from ~65% to >90%, 20–30% lower packaging cost versus organic substrates, and support for AI packages >14x reticle size; separately Intel's EMIB-T is reported at >90% yield, mass production 2027, and roughly half the cost of CoWoS, having already won a MediaTek AI ASIC order with Broadcom and Meta evaluating (CoWoS interposer is capped near 3.3x reticle, CoWoS-L 3.5x). The named beneficiaries are Taiwan mid-caps (Grand Plastics on TGV wet process, Wanrun, Chun Hua; PSMC as Intel's sole silicon-capacitor supplier with capacity fully booked, UMC on silicon bridges), and PSMC's Aug 4 presser confirmed strategy and interposer/Si-Cap roadmap continuity after founder Frank Huang's death. For coverage this is the copos-panel and glass-substrate questions turning from research topic to dated commercialization schedule: a credible ~50%-cheaper, larger-reticle alternative caps the CoWoS pricing umbrella TSM currently enjoys and gives INTC a genuine foundry/packaging wedge (intel-18a), while >14x reticle capability is what future multi-die XPUs from AVGO/MRVL will need. The AI section is also relevant to the cost curve: Alibaba's 2.4T-parameter Qwen3.8-Max priced at ~40%/24% of Claude Opus 5 input/output, all top-five OpenRouter models now Chinese, and DeepSeek V4-Flash at under 1% of Fable 5's cost per test reportedly forcing an 80% price cut on OpenAI's GPT-5.6 Luna in late July — inference-token deflation that is bullish for compute volume but bearish for any model-layer margin thesis. Confirm via a TSMC CoPoS validation milestone or an EMIB-T customer beyond MediaTek; refute if substrate yields (see the conflicting figure in the Nystedt item) stall 2027 MP.
  Sources: [techsoda.substack.com](https://techsoda.substack.com/p/global-market-watch-advanced-packaging)
- `8` **MediaTek Advances CoWoS, EMIB-T and 3.5D Packaging Strategy - Converge Digest**
  The sharpest custom-silicon datapoint in this batch: on its Q2 call MediaTek raised its target share of the 2027 AI accelerator silicon market to 15–20% from 10–15% of a stated $80B TAM (excluding HBM, CPUs and switches), said its first AI ASIC enters production in Q4 2026 with datacenter revenue >$2B this year, and kept gen-2 (larger, more powerful) on track for initial production in early 2028 with both generations shipping concurrently through 2028. Mechanism disclosed: dual-track back-end across TSMC CoWoS *and* Intel EMIB-T (Rick Tsai: EMIB-T yields and maturity now 'more constructive' for volume — corroborating item 0's 90% claim), 3.5D integration, 2nm 448G SerDes available 2H27, co-packaged copper as the near-term short-reach solution, CPO on TSMC COUPE later, and pre-validated subsystems that include NVLink Fusion connectivity. For the XPU share question this is a direct threat to MRVL and AVGO: MediaTek raising its share target by 5pts of an $80B TAM (~$4B) is share that must come from incumbent ASIC vendors, and the NVLink Fusion integration plus 'discussions with several prospective customers' says MediaTek is bidding beyond its anchor account (mrvl-fabric, openai-xpu adjacency). Also note CFO David Ku's comment that the industry is evaluating alternative memory architectures because of HBM supply constraints while HBM remains necessary for top-end accelerators — a demand-tightness confirmation for 000660/285A and a partial validation of item 4's premise. Confirm/refute via MediaTek naming a second hyperscaler, or via MRVL's next guide on custom-ASIC socket wins; co-packaged copper before CPO also implies CPO revenue timing slips toward 2028 for this platform (LITE, AAOI read-through).
  Sources: [convergedigest.com](https://convergedigest.com/mediatek-advances-cowos-emib-t-and-3-5d-packaging-strategy)
- `8` **High Bandwidth Flash für extrem schnellen KI-Speicher ist fertig | heise online**
  The first HBF (High Bandwidth Flash) technical specification is published inside the Open Compute Project: up to 512GB per stack (vs HBM4's max ~48GB), three speed tiers from 400GB/s to 3TB/s, four stacks exceeding 10TB/s (HBM4 tops out at ~3.3TB/s per stack), 16 stacked NAND dies (>5,000 layers), attached over UCIe. The catch is NAND latency, which is why SK hynix frames HBF as a new tier between HBM and datacenter SSDs — coexisting on the accelerator — while Sandisk, which has no DRAM, pushes it as the AI gold standard; heise notes accelerators shipping with HBF are still several years out. For coverage: this creates a credible non-DRAM sink for high-layer-count NAND bits, a structural demand argument for SNDK and a second revenue vector for 000660's NAND business, and it sits alongside Credo's Weaver as evidence the inference-capacity bottleneck is being attacked outside HBM. The 'several years' timeline means this is a narrative/multiple event now, not a 2026–27 volume event; confirmation would be an accelerator vendor (Google, Tenstorrent) publicly committing HBF into a shipping product roadmap.
  Sources: [www.heise.de](https://www.heise.de/news/High-Bandwidth-Flash-fuer-extrem-schnellen-KI-Speicher-ist-fertig-11398882.html)
- `8` **Sandisk Releases First OCP High Bandwidth Flash Spec | SNDK Stock News**
  Sandisk and SK hynix released the HBF spec through OCP just six months after the February consortium launch — fast for a memory standard — with Google and Tenstorrent joining as members and contributing validation; the spec defines the xPU–HBF host interface, electrical, reliability and die-stack packaging guidance plus a read/write software guide, explicitly enabling HBF to coexist with HBM. The strategic intent is stated openly: publish first to make HBF the de facto standard in AI storage and pull an ecosystem forward. Google's membership is the most decision-useful fact here, as a hyperscaler validating a NAND-based near-compute tier is what converts HBF from a Sandisk marketing thesis into a bit-demand forecast for SNDK and 000660's NAND line; Tenstorrent's presence suggests first adoption comes from challenger accelerators rather than NVDA/AMD. Near-term catalysts: Sandisk's FMS keynote Aug 5 11:40am PT (Elliott/Ismail/Ilkbahar) and the Aug 6 Sandisk–SK hynix–Google panel, where any capacity/timeline or customer specifics would be the first hard datapoint.
  Sources: [stocktitan.net](https://stocktitan.net/news/SNDK/sandisk-and-sk-hynix-advance-global-standardization-of-high-8hd8me1m63lf.html)
- `8` **Aehr Stock Jumps as Silicon Photonics Order Signals Manufacturing Scale-Up - Aehr Test System (NASDAQ:AEH - Benzinga**
  Aehr received a production order for an automated multi-wafer burn-in system from a silicon photonics customer expanding silicon photonic integrated circuit output for optical transceivers and chip-to-chip optical I/O; the configuration includes nine WaferPak blades at up to 3,500W each plus automated alignment/handling, with shipment expected in 1H 2027 — so revenue recognition sits in FY27, not near-term. CEO Gayn Erickson framed it as silicon photonics 'moving from technology adoption into manufacturing scale-up,' with demand spanning optical switching, co-packaged optics and linear pluggable optics; the economic mechanism is screening defects at wafer level before packaging, which raises yield and lowers cost on expensive SiPh die. This is a direct read on both wlbi-test (AEHR) and the cpo adoption-timing question: an automated multi-wafer production tool (versus engineering-volume systems) implies a customer committing to volume SiPh capacity roughly 2027, consistent with CPO ramping rather than piloting, which supports LITE, IQE, AAOI and the InP/photonics complex on timing. Shares +16.8% at $107.15 — 25.7% above the 20-day SMA and 87.8% above the 200-day — so positioning is stretched into the news; confirmation would be the customer being named or follow-on WaferPak orders, refutation would be slippage of the 1H27 shipment or no repeat order, and note the order itself gives no dollar value in the text.
  Sources: [benzinga.com](https://benzinga.com/markets/tech/26/08/60914065/aehr-stock-jumps-as-silicon-photonics-order-signals-manufacturing-scale-up)
- `7` **SK hynix Unveils 375-Layer NAND With 2.5x Performance per Watt - Stock Titan**
  SK hynix unveiled a 375-layer NAND with a claimed 2.5x performance-per-watt improvement, a step up from its current 321-layer generation. Headline only — no pricing, capacity or timing — so read it as a cost-per-bit and power-efficiency lever rather than a supply event: higher layer counts cut $/GB and are the prerequisite for NAND to serve AI inference tiering (adjacent to the HBF standard items). - `6` **Cloudflare Wallets and x402: How AI Agents Pay for APIs**
  Cloudflare announced Cloudflare Wallets on Aug 4, 2026 — Account Wallets held by humans, Virtual Wallets delegated to agents via API key with owner-set hard spend caps, weekly budgets, allow-lists, anomaly detection and a human override path, plus `cloudflare.pay` handles built on Web Bot Auth — pairing with the seller-side Monetization Gateway announced ~Jul 1, 2026. Settlement is stablecoin over x402, i.e. payment attached to an ordinary HTTP request via a 402 response, so the payment itself becomes the credential and no prior buyer-seller relationship or signup is needed. Critically, neither half ships today: Wallets payments are "coming soon" and the Gateway is waitlist-only, so this is protocol land-grab, not revenue, for NET. For the agentic-commerce/net-act4 questions the read is that Cloudflare is trying to convert its bot-traffic chokepoint (majority of web traffic now bot-driven) from a blocking product into a toll rail with a take-rate; confirmation would be GA dates, disclosed fee economics and named merchants/labs settling volume, while continued waitlist status into 2027 would mean x402 remains a standards story that Stripe/card networks can still preempt.

- `4` **Sandisk y SK Hynix publican la primera especificación técnica para el estándar de la memoria HBF**
  Sandisk and SK hynix published the first HBF (High Bandwidth Flash) technical specification through the Open Compute Project on 2026-08-03 — roughly six months after the consortium formally began work in February, a fast standardization cadence — with Google and Tenstorrent named as additional members and a debut session plus panel at FMS this week. The spec defines technical/electrical design guidelines plus a read/write user guide, and is openly available without restriction, which is the precondition for accelerator vendors to design HBF into silicon rather than treat it as a single-vendor experiment. For SNDK and 000660 this is the key hbf-flash datapoint: an OCP spec plus Google's participation moves HBF from partnership PR toward a demand-side pull on NAND positioned between DRAM and SSD, which if it lands would give Sandisk a structurally higher-value NAND mix (implies pricing power on capacity-tier AI memory) and gives SK hynix an adjacency to its HBM franchise rather than a cannibalization risk. Confirmation would be a named accelerator with HBF in its memory hierarchy or a capacity/capex commitment tied to HBF bit output; refutation would be the spec stalling at v1 with no silicon partner beyond Tenstorrent, since spec publication alone carries no revenue. Note the text names Google as a standardization member only — no purchase or design-in is claimed.

- `6` **삼전 · 하닉 , 美 FMS 2026서 AI 메모리 해법 대거 공개** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  Adds mechanism detail to the FMS 2026 memory-roadmap fight: the SK hynix/SanDisk HBF standard was published through OCP as an open spec and uses UCIe as the chiplet interface to processors, so HBF can attach flexibly to GPUs, CPUs and custom xPUs — with Google and Tenstorrent already in the consortium. SK hynix's keynote frames a tiered stack of 3D-stacked DRAM on the xPU, HBF, and CXL memory pooling for agentic-AI inference; Samsung's counter is z-NAND plus zHBM (DRAM stacked on top of the GPU rather than beside it), explicitly leveraging its IDM combination of design, foundry and advanced packaging. Two coverage implications: (1) UCIe + OCP openness plus Google/Tenstorrent participation is the credible-adoption signal for hbf-flash and lifts SNDK from NAND-cycle beta toward an AI-tier supplier (and dovetails with MRVL's Structera/Photonic Fabric disaggregation pitch); (2) zHBM's GPU-stacked structure requires hybrid bonding/advanced packaging, tying Samsung's HBM4-era competitiveness to its own packaging execution and reading across to hybrid-bonding and CoWoS-alternative capacity questions. Confirmation: HBF spec v1 bandwidth/capacity numbers and any hyperscaler committing silicon; refutation: no controller ecosystem or JEDEC follow-through, leaving HBF an OCP whitepaper.

For 000660 this reframes 'record results' as share loss into the strongest pricing environment in years — the bull case now depends entirely on the Q3 HBM4 ramp restoring mix, which Counterpoint expects to slow HBM price declines and turn them up in 2027. One extra policy datapoint: the proposed Korean production tax credit is assessed as mainly benefiting domestic materials/components/equipment suppliers, since the domestic production-and-sale requirement and no-overlap-with-investment-credit rule leave Samsung and hynix (>90% export) with limited benefit — i.e., no incremental earnings support for 000660 from that measure. - `6` **Wall Street Lunch: Ban Talk Lifts Networking Names**
  The Trump administration, via the FCC, is drafting an import ban on Chinese-made optical transceivers for US data centers on malware/data-collection grounds, with possible publication before year-end — following the FCC's precedent bans on Chinese drones, inverters, robots and routers. The mechanism matters: Suzhou-based InnoLight is the world's #1 transceiver supplier with Google, Meta and Nvidia as customers, so a ban forcibly re-sources a large share of 800G/1.6T module volume to Western vendors — the tape agreed, with COHR +13%, LITE +7%, CIEN +5%, INTC +8%, AVGO +4%, CSCO +3%. For coverage this is a potential inflection, not incremental: it is bullish pricing power and volume for LITE and the InP/EML supply chain (IQE, inp-capacity question), supportive of TSEM's SiPho foundry guide the same day, and it raises the strategic value of co-packaged optics roadmaps at AVGO/NVDA where the Chinese module layer is designed out entirely. Confirmation would be an actual FCC NPRM with a covered-entity list and effective dates; refutation would be a narrow scope (government/federal data centers only) or long grandfathering, which would blunt the re-sourcing volume — and note the risk that Chinese retaliation touches EML/laser or rare-earth inputs.

- `4` **Robinhood Gets UK's Permission to Offer Crypto Services**
  Robinhood was added to the FCA's registered cryptoasset firms list effective July 31, clearing it to offer crypto services in the UK ahead of the broader UK digital-asset regime that takes effect October 2027 (FCA oversight limited to financial promotions and AML). This lands on top of last week's record quarter — $1.3B revenue, +32% y/y, with new highs in equities, options and prediction markets — and management framing the next phase as owning more of the transaction stack (tokenized assets, banking, cards) rather than more trades. For HOOD the transmission is geographic TAM plus take-rate mix: UK crypto adds a high-margin revenue line and pre-positions HOOD before rivals complete registration, while BTC-CRYPTO price levels (quoted ~$64.1k, +1.0%) remain the swing factor on crypto volumes. The agentic-trading product — described as still buggy — is the item that touches the agentic-commerce research question: if agent-initiated trades and card payments scale, HOOD becomes a rails story; confirmation would be disclosed agentic transaction volumes or UK crypto revenue broken out next quarter.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMiwwFBVV95cUxPMzRXMVlYaTFWUkMtZVdMMTRkazdpS2Zuazd0WUxhMWFfTy05OENXV0doU2ZZbEdqMklJZ0EzVnlHdVI0QTBFTmJUckZBUk5LS1JCNHVlNjh6WE96X1RkSnNfY3Iwa1g5QTdUOE1NeXczZ2V1RHo5QjlOSlkwSFVrRTZvX2NuZTRHYW1jT0QzX280bWloZzdBVVc5Wm8tdUVBM1hTWDNkMXlTTTJMV2pZcXdDVkllZkhhOHBhenV2aGQ0NlU?oc=5) · [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMinwFBVV95cUxNaVc0QzdiMjFXd0ctX1JKeEdVUGJjZlNoT1VwMWlxNWw0WkZRRERVSEYtNjNDYzY0ZV9USEN4MFhwNEVFMk13Q2pzWTEyZmR6SlV4WG9pSTE4MUppNE5HQ0ZzZXdJTFBOMnNwV2ZkMDNjVGVPMGdTMEtWV3JLYmloY1lfcmR1MnZPUzBkTXdQemdJOXlGVHh6aGxkTm9TbTA?oc=5) · [pymnts.com](https://www.pymnts.com/cryptocurrency/2026/robinhood-gets-united-kingdom-permission-offer-crypto-services/)
- `4` **Networking stocks surge as Trump drafts ban on Chinese data center component: report | Seeking Alpha** → [[Theses/AVGO - Broadcom]]
  Report that the Trump administration is drafting a ban on Chinese-made data center components sent US networking names higher, with Cisco, Broadcom (AVGO) and Intel (INTC) cited as beneficiaries and China's InnoLight — the leading merchant 800G/1.6T optical transceiver supplier — plus its customers as losers. If enacted, this is the single largest addressable share-shift event for Western optics in the coverage list: InnoLight/Eoptolink displacement would route volume to AAOI, LITE (module and EML capacity) and Coherent, and reinforce the vertical CPO path at AVGO/MRVL, touching the export-controls and china-optics questions. Text is a headline summary only — no draft scope, component definitions, or effective date — so treat magnitude as unquantified; confirmation would be a published BIS/Commerce rule text naming transceivers or switch subassemblies, refutation would be the usual dilution to a narrow procurement restriction on federal buyers. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [seekingalpha.com](https://seekingalpha.com/news/4624587-networking-stocks-surge-as-trump-drafts-ban-on-chinese-data-center-component-report)
- `3` **SEMICON West names Phoenix as long-term host - AZ Big Media** → [[Theses/AMAT - Applied Materials]]
  SEMI named Phoenix the long-term home of SEMICON West and moved it to spring, starting Mar 30–Apr 1, 2027, after the Arizona debut drew a 60% jump in registrations and ~50% more exhibitors — the best-attended SEMICON West in nearly 20 years. The embedded hard datapoint for tsmc-nodes/tsmc-capex: TSMC's July announcement of an additional $100B in Arizona for four more 2nm-or-more-advanced fabs takes its total Arizona commitment to $265B, within a state that has drawn >70 semiconductor expansions and >$314B of investment since 2020, with Intel, ASML, Applied Materials and Amkor also expanding there. Transmission is capacity and cost curve: four incremental leading-edge fabs on US soil is N2/A16 capacity that lands in the same 2027-2030 window as the $860B-to-$1.2T hyperscaler capex path, and it pulls forward US tool orders and advanced-packaging/OSAT localization (AMAT, LRCX, KLA, ONTO, ASMI, TER, plus Amkor-adjacent packaging). The conference relocation itself is a sentiment/ecosystem marker, not a fundamental; the confirmable items are TSMC's Arizona construction schedule and whether the $100B shows up in raised group capex guidance rather than being spread over a decade.
  Sources: [azbigmedia.com](https://azbigmedia.com/business/technology/semicon-west-names-phoenix-as-long-term-host)
- `3` **Palantir's Q2 Earnings Rally Reinforces Its Strategic Role In The Global Order**
  Palantir's Q2 raised FY26 revenue guidance to $8.15B, with US commercial revenue +149% y/y, net dollar retention 157%, and non-cancelable contracted revenue doubling to $4.9B — the backlog metric is the most decision-useful because it converts the AIP narrative into visibility rather than pipeline. The stock's +24% move (per the same day's market wrap) puts it at roughly 50x 2026 sales and 84x adjusted operating income on this author's math, so the multiple, not the fundamentals, is now the variable — any AI-sector or rate reset hits PLTR hardest. Touches pltr-defense/pltr-rivals: the 149% US commercial rate implies share capture against Microsoft Fabric IQ / Genie-style entrants is not yet visible in Palantir's numbers, and a doubling of non-cancelable revenue argues incumbency is deepening. Watch Q3 guidance and whether US government growth (NGC2/Army) keeps pace with commercial; a deceleration in US commercial below ~100% or flat non-cancelable backlog would refute the compounding read at this valuation.
  Sources: [seekingalpha.com](https://seekingalpha.com/article/4930167-palantir-q2-earnings-rally-reinforces-its-strategic-role-in-the-global-order)

## value

- `2` **Spending Money**
  Ben Carlson's macro note argues US consumer spending remains strong despite sentiment: full bars/restaurants on a Tuesday in Chicago at $19 cocktails, late-July setting the busiest commercial air-traffic day in history, the biggest-ever single box-office weekend, and a WSJ-sourced boating datapoint of $20k–$70k+ outboards installed in pairs or quads (Raymond James' Joe Altobello attributes it to upscale buyers' soaring stock portfolios). He also flags unemployment below 5% for nearly five years (8–9 years if you bridge the Covid spike) with prime-age participation above any point in the 2010s. For coverage this is background support, not an inflection: the affluent-discretionary read is mildly positive for LYV (live events), MTN (destination resorts), ONON and UBER, and the wealth-effect channel implies these cohorts' spend is now stock-market-levered — so a drawdown in the AI complex would transmit to discretionary names, not just semis. Nothing here is company-specific or actionable for the AI-supply-chain priorities; refuted if payrolls/unemployment break above 5% or if the box-office/air-traffic records prove calendar artifacts.
  Sources: [Ben Carlson](https://awealthofcommonsense.com/2026/08/spending-money/)

  13F roundup showing institutional buying in Eli Lilly: AQR +348,581 shares to 855,420 ($0.79B), Fisher +232,659 to 4,838,986 ($4.45B, the largest holder listed), Point72 +28,020, GMO +10,541, Gotham +4,867, GAMCO +1,463, with Bridgewater modestly trimming. LLY is not in the coverage universe and no read-through to AI supply chain, memory, or semicap names; backward-looking filing data with no catalyst.

- `0` **Why Adobe Could Be One of the Cheapest Quality Stocks Today**
  Podcast excerpt (Travis/Carlisle) claiming Adobe trades at roughly nine times forward earnings on some estimates, with AI-disruption fear driving elevated options implied volatility that lets them harvest 20–30% annualized premiums on cash-secured puts/covered calls. Adobe is not a coverage ticker, but the setup is the same "AI kills incumbent software" derating the investor is watching in INTU (frontier-lab agents entering tax/bookkeeping) and to a degree NOW/PCOR/INTU multiples generally — implies the market is already pricing terminal-value risk into application software rather than waiting for revenue evidence. No new data on agent capability or share loss here, so treat as sentiment color only; confirmation would be actual seat/retention disclosures from an incumbent.
  Sources: [Johnny Hopkins](https://acquirersmultiple.com/2026/08/why-adobe-could-be-one-of-the-cheapest-quality-stocks-today/)
- `0` **Meme Stocks, Gambling, and America’s Risk-Taking Culture**
  Meb Faber podcast excerpt on America's risk-taking culture, arguing today's difference versus historical manias (1720s Europe, 1873) is accessibility — phone-based in-play betting and single-day options — plus a novel status inversion where traders brag about total losses on Reddit. Relevant only as retail-speculation-regime color for HOOD (options/event-contract volumes) and crypto-linked names (BTC-CRYPTO, CRCL, IREN); implies the retail engagement that drives HOOD's transaction take rate is culturally entrenched rather than a one-off, but there are no volume, account, or revenue figures here to size it.
  Sources: [Johnny Hopkins](https://acquirersmultiple.com/2026/08/meme-stocks-gambling-and-americas-risk-taking-culture/)
